# Proof Debt Ledger

Auto-generated by `(proof-debt-ledger)`.  283 proven theorem(s); each is certified by the kernel relative
to the `asserted` facts listed as its outstanding base.

## Reading an entry

A proof's **bill** is the set of `asserted` facts it ultimately
leans on -- resolved transitively: a cited *proven* lemma
contributes its own bill, a *primitive* axiom or *definitional*
fact contributes nothing, and an *asserted* fact contributes
itself.  Each such fact is a **leaf** of the bill.

- **`proven modulo 0`** (an *empty bill*) -- the proof rests only
  on the trusted base (the fixed primitive axioms, the kernel
  inference rules, and definitions).  Nothing asserted stands
  between it and the kernel; this is the strongest thing a proof
  can report, and it carries **no** trust tier.
- **`proven modulo {a, b, ...}`** -- the proof is unconditional
  *given* a, b, ...  Discharging every leaf (proving it, or making
  it a definition) would collapse the bill to 0.

The **trust tier** `[trust: K]` of a non-empty bill is the warrant
of its **weakest** leaf -- one shaky leaf caps the whole proof.
Tiers, weakest to strongest (from `*warrant-kinds*`; see the
manual, ch. Classification):

| tier | the weakest leaf is... |
|------|------------------------|
| **`none`** | asserted with **no warrant at all** -- a backlog item, not a category |
| `hand-wave` | a heuristic / plausibility argument, not rigorous |
| `well-known` | a standard textbook fact, asserted without argument |
| `reference` | cites a specific named source |
| `informal` | a rigorous paper-proof exists, just not mechanised in VNB |
| `proof` | a machine-checked VNB proof exists |

So `[trust: none]` is the **weakest** report (some leaf justifies
nothing), and `modulo 0` -- no tier -- is the strongest.

### binomial-theorem  *(trust: well-known)*

*trusted code: `crs`* -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

proven modulo:

- `bt-one-in-carr` -- *warrant well-known:* ring ONE lies in CARR.
- `comb-kk-0-0` -- *warrant well-known:* comb-kk-zero + IF(0=0) picks the then-branch ONE(R).
- `comb-kk-in-fun` -- *warrant well-known:* Each COMB-KK(R,x,y,m) is a total function ZZ -> CARR R: base is IF into
   {ONE,ZERO}; step is a sum of products of carrier elements.  Induction on m.
- `bt-succ-in-nn` -- *warrant well-known:* succ closes NN.
- `bt-neg1-in-zz` -- *warrant well-known:* -1 in ZZ.
- `bt-nn-in-zz` -- *warrant well-known:* NN subset ZZ.
- `bt-add-in-carr` -- *warrant well-known:* curried ring-carrier-closed-add.
- `bt-sum-in-carr-zz` -- *warrant well-known:* SUM over the NN indices of a ZZ-fn lands in CARR.
- `bt-neg1-neg` -- *warrant well-known:* -1 < 0.
- `bt-lt-succ` -- *warrant well-known:* n < succ n.
- `comb-kk-null` -- *warrant well-known:* k<0 => COMB-KK(.,m)(k)=ZERO.  Induction on m: base IF(k=0) is ZERO for k<0;
   step x*val(k-1)+y*val(k) with k-1<0 and k<0 both ZERO by IH.
- `comb-kk-above` -- *warrant well-known:* k>m => COMB-KK(.,m)(k)=ZERO.  Induction on m: base k>0 => IF(k=0) is ZERO;
   step k>succ m => both k-1>m and k>m, ZERO by IH.

### block-family-combinatorial  *(trust: none)*

proven modulo:

- `cover-block-step` -- *warrant well-known:* Infinite pigeonhole behind a finite cover.  C finite covers V, so CHOICE
   gives a classifier pi:J->C with f(i) in pi(i) for i in J; pigeonhole-
   infinite on the infinite J then yields a member U in C with an infinite
   fibre J_ = SEP(i in J | pi(i)=U) subset J, and f(i) in U for every i in
   J_.  Metric-free generalization of tb-block-step (which is this at C = a
   finite rad-net's ball cover).
- `inf-subsets-is-set` -- **NO WARRANT**
- `nn-in-inf-subsets` -- *warrant well-known:* NN is an infinite subset of NN: NN subset NN and CARD(NN) not in NN.
- `dc-on-nn-pred` -- *warrant reference:* Set-valued-step form of dc-on-nn (same file).  Derivable from primitive
   recursion on NN + global Hilbert choice: g(k,u):=CHOICE{y in X:y in nxt
   (k,u)} is defined by the totality hypothesis, then recurse f(0):=a,
   f(succ k):=g(k,f k); f:NN->X by induction.  No relation-set encoding --
   nxt(k,u) is the successor SET, supplied as a VNB-LAMBDA, so callers avoid
   the (LIST k u y)-in-R comprehension/sethood/NTH plumbing (O1 of
   archive/calculus-pre-rename/block-family-rederive.scm).  Accepted asserted during library-build.

### border-identity  *(trust: well-known)*

proven modulo:

- `ring-one-in` -- *warrant well-known:* ONE lies in the carrier.
- `identmat-type` -- *warrant reference:* IDENTMAT(A,n) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A (its entries are ONE/ZERO of A).
- `border-type` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q) in MAT(succ p, succ q, CARR A): each tabulator value is b
   (in CARR A), ZERO A (in CARR A), or b block entry M_{i-1,j-1} (entry-in-carrier,
   i-1 in [1,p] for i in [2,succ p]); matof-in-mat applies.
- `entry-of-identmat` -- *warrant reference:* (IDENTMAT A n)_{ij} = 1 if i=j else 0 (entry-of-matof on the Kronecker delta).
- `border-entry-11` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{1,1} = b (i=j=1 branch).
- `border-entry-block2` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{i,j} = M_{i-1,j-1} for i,j /= 1 in the border range (the
   i/=1,j/=1 branch of the def; NN-MINUS = monus predecessor).
- `pred-in-interval` -- *warrant well-known:* i in [2, succ p] => i-1 in [1, p]: the monus predecessor of an index past 1
   lands in the block range.
- `border-entry-1j` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{1,j} = 0 for j /= 1 (i=1, j/=1 branch).
- `border-entry-i1` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{i,1} = 0 for i /= 1 (i/=1, j=1 branch).
- `nn-minus-1-inj` -- *warrant well-known:* i /= j and 1 <= i,j => i-1 /= j-1 (monus by 1 is injective on [1,inf)).
- `matrix-entry-extensionality` -- *warrant well-known:* Two m-by-n matrices over X with equal entries are equal (tuple/list
   extensionality applied row- and entry-wise).

### border-invertible  *(trust: well-known)*

proven modulo:

- `ring-one-in` -- *warrant well-known:* ONE lies in the carrier.
- `border-type` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q) in MAT(succ p, succ q, CARR A): each tabulator value is b
   (in CARR A), ZERO A (in CARR A), or b block entry M_{i-1,j-1} (entry-in-carrier,
   i-1 in [1,p] for i in [2,succ p]); matof-in-mat applies.
- `identmat-type` -- *warrant reference:* IDENTMAT(A,n) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A (its entries are ONE/ZERO of A).
- `matmul-type` -- *warrant reference:* MAT(m,n,CARR A) x MAT(n,k,CARR A) -> MAT(m,k,CARR A): the tabulated product
   is a matrix whose entries are FINSUMs in the carrier (matof-in-mat +
   finsum-type).
- `ring-carrier-closed-mul` -- *warrant proof:* MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).
- `interval-card-in-nn` -- *warrant well-known:* INTERVAL(a,b) is finite, so its cardinality is a natural number.
- `one-in-interval` -- *warrant well-known:* 1 in [1, succ n] (1 <= 1 <= succ n).
- `matmul-entry` -- *warrant reference:* (P Q)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij}*Q_{jc}, the product summed in A's additive
   group over j in 1..n (entry-of-matof on the MATMUL tabulation).
- `matprod-summand-type` -- *warrant well-known:* j |-> P_{ij}.Q_{jc} is a function [1,n] -> CARR A for P:MAT(m,n), Q:MAT(n,nn)
   (entry-in-carrier gives both factors in CARR A; MUL closes; CARR(RAG)=CARR A).
- `border-entry-1j` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{1,j} = 0 for j /= 1 (i=1, j/=1 branch).
- `entry-in-carrier` -- *warrant well-known:* The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
- `ring-mul-zero-left` -- *warrant well-known:* 0.a = 0: a.0 = a.(0+0) = a.0 + a.0, cancel a.0 in the additive group.  Lang, Algebra II.1.
- `ras-id` -- *warrant proof:* identity of a ring's additive group is the ring's zero.
- `finsum-single-support` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(j)=0 for every j in the finite S except j=i0, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=f(i0).
- `border-entry-11` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{1,1} = b (i=j=1 branch).
- `ring-mul-zero-right` -- *warrant well-known:* a.0 = 0: symmetric to ring-mul-zero-left.  Lang, Algebra II.1.
- `border-entry-i1` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{i,1} = 0 for i /= 1 (i/=1, j=1 branch).
- `funcomp-succ-type` -- *warrant well-known:* z |-> f(succ z) : [1,q] -> X when f : [1,succ q] -> X (succ z in [1,succ q]).
- `finsum-interval-shift` -- *warrant well-known:* FINSUM over [1,succ n] = f(1) . FINSUM over [1,n] of (z|->f(succ z)): peel the
   first term (finsum-insert-ag at k=1, X=[2,succ n]) then reindex the tail by the
   succ bijection [1,n]->[2,succ n] (finsum-reindex-ag).  Standard finite-sum
   front-peel, same status as finsum-ord-peel.
- `pred-in-interval` -- *warrant well-known:* i in [2, succ p] => i-1 in [1, p]: the monus predecessor of an index past 1
   lands in the block range.
- `border-entry-block2` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{i,j} = M_{i-1,j-1} for i,j /= 1 in the border range (the
   i/=1,j/=1 branch of the def; NN-MINUS = monus predecessor).
- `succ-in-interval` -- *warrant well-known:* z in [1,q] => succ z in [1, succ q] (2 <= succ z <= succ q).
- `succ-not-one` -- *warrant well-known:* z in [1,q] => succ z >= 2, so succ z /= 1.
- `nn-minus-succ-1` -- *warrant well-known:* NN-MINUS(succ z, 1) = z: 1 <= succ z, so the monus is (succ z) - 1 = z
   (nn-minus-def + bt-succ-minus-1).
- `finsum-congruence` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=
   FINSUM(ag,g,S).  Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees
   (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH.  Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on
   the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
- `ras-op` -- *warrant proof:* operation of a ring's additive group is the ring's addition.
- `finsum-type` -- *warrant informal:* Closure of the finite sum on the carrier.  Induction on card(S): the fold
   seeds at (IDEN ag), in (CARR ag) by the identity law, and each step applies
   (OPR ag), which closes on (CARR ag) by the group's binary-operation typing.
   No inverses used -- the same argument as finsum-comm-monoid-type.
- `ras-carr` -- *warrant proof:* carrier of a ring's additive group is the ring's carrier.
- `matrix-entry-extensionality` -- *warrant well-known:* Two m-by-n matrices over X with equal entries are equal (tuple/list
   extensionality applied row- and entry-wise).
- `entry-of-identmat` -- *warrant reference:* (IDENTMAT A n)_{ij} = 1 if i=j else 0 (entry-of-matof on the Kronecker delta).
- `nn-minus-1-inj` -- *warrant well-known:* i /= j and 1 <= i,j => i-1 /= j-1 (monus by 1 is injective on [1,inf)).

   (entry-in-carrier gives both factors in CARR A; MUL closes; CARR(RAG)=CARR A).

- `entry-in-carrier` -- *warrant well-known:* The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
- `ring-mul-zero-left` -- *warrant well-known:* 0.a = 0: a.0 = a.(0+0) = a.0 + a.0, cancel a.0 in the additive group.  Lang, Algebra II.1.
- `ras-id` -- *warrant proof:* identity of a ring's additive group is the ring's zero.
- `finsum-single-support` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(j)=0 for every j in the finite S except j=i0, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=f(i0).
- `border-entry-11` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{1,1} = b (i=j=1 branch).
- `ring-mul-zero-right` -- *warrant well-known:* a.0 = 0: symmetric to ring-mul-zero-left.  Lang, Algebra II.1.
- `border-entry-i1` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{i,1} = 0 for i /= 1 (i/=1, j=1 branch).
- `funcomp-succ-type` -- *warrant well-known:* z |-> f(succ z) : [1,q] -> X when f : [1,succ q] -> X (succ z in [1,succ q]).
- `finsum-interval-shift` -- *warrant well-known:* FINSUM over [1,succ n] = f(1) . FINSUM over [1,n] of (z|->f(succ z)): peel the
   first term (finsum-insert-ag at k=1, X=[2,succ n]) then reindex the tail by the
   succ bijection [1,n]->[2,succ n] (finsum-reindex-ag).  Standard finite-sum
   front-peel, same status as finsum-ord-peel.
- `pred-in-interval` -- *warrant well-known:* i in [2, succ p] => i-1 in [1, p]: the monus predecessor of an index past 1
   lands in the block range.
- `border-entry-block2` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{i,j} = M_{i-1,j-1} for i,j /= 1 in the border range (the
   i/=1,j/=1 branch of the def; NN-MINUS = monus predecessor).
- `succ-in-interval` -- *warrant well-known:* z in [1,q] => succ z in [1, succ q] (2 <= succ z <= succ q).
- `succ-not-one` -- *warrant well-known:* z in [1,q] => succ z >= 2, so succ z /= 1.
- `nn-minus-succ-1` -- *warrant well-known:* NN-MINUS(succ z, 1) = z: 1 <= succ z, so the monus is (succ z) - 1 = z
   (nn-minus-def + bt-succ-minus-1).
- `finsum-congruence` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=
   FINSUM(ag,g,S).  Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees
   (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH.  Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on
   the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
- `ras-op` -- *warrant proof:* operation of a ring's additive group is the ring's addition.
- `finsum-type` -- *warrant informal:* Closure of the finite sum on the carrier.  Induction on card(S): the fold
   seeds at (IDEN ag), in (CARR ag) by the identity law, and each step applies
   (OPR ag), which closes on (CARR ag) by the group's binary-operation typing.
   No inverses used -- the same argument as finsum-comm-monoid-type.
- `ras-carr` -- *warrant proof:* carrier of a ring's additive group is the ring's carrier.
- `matrix-entry-extensionality` -- *warrant well-known:* Two m-by-n matrices over X with equal entries are equal (tuple/list
   extensionality applied row- and entry-wise).
- `entry-of-identmat` -- *warrant reference:* (IDENTMAT A n)_{ij} = 1 if i=j else 0 (entry-of-matof on the Kronecker delta).
- `nn-minus-1-inj` -- *warrant well-known:* i /= j and 1 <= i,j => i-1 /= j-1 (monus by 1 is injective on [1,inf)).

- `finsum-single-support` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(j)=0 for every j in the finite S except j=i0, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=f(i0).
- `elem-g-ro-at` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{ii} = 1 for i/=k (identity row diagonal).
- `elem-g-entry-l-vanish` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{jc} = 0 when c=l and j is neither l (diagonal) nor k (r-slot).
- `ring-mul-zero-right` -- *warrant well-known:* a.0 = 0: symmetric to ring-mul-zero-left.  Lang, Algebra II.1.
- `elem-g-entry-l-at-l` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{lc} = 1 when c=l (the diagonal entry of column l).
- `elem-g-entry-l-at-k` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{kc} = r when c=l and k/=l (the r-slot in column l).
- `elem-g-entry-off-l-off-diag` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{jc} = 0 when c/=l and j/=c (off the diagonal, off the r-slot).
- `elem-g-entry-off-l-diag` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{cc} = 1 when c/=l (the diagonal, r-slot inactive).
- `entry-of-identmat` -- *warrant reference:* (IDENTMAT A n)_{ij} = 1 if i=j else 0 (entry-of-matof on the Kronecker delta).
- `elem-g-col-l` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{il} = 1 if i=l, r if i=k, else 0 (column l: diagonal + the r-slot).
- `elem-g-col-k` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{ik} = 1 if i=k else 0, when k/=l (column k is an identity column).
- `ring-one-in` -- *warrant well-known:* ONE lies in the carrier.
- `ring-add-right-id` -- *warrant well-known:* a + 0 = a (ring-add-left-id + comm).
- `ring-add-right-inv` -- *warrant well-known:* a + (-a) = 0 (ring-add-left-inv + comm).
- `elem-g-col-other` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{ic} = 1 if i=c else 0, when c/=l (any non-l column is identity).
- `ring-neg-neg` -- *warrant well-known:* -(-r) = r in any ring: r's additive inverse's inverse is r (group double-inverse).
- `nn-succ-le-antisym` -- *warrant well-known:* succ a <= b => not(b <= a) for a,b in NN: succ a <= b gives a < b, so b <= a
   would give a < a.  Elementary order on NN.
- `nn-le-succ-cases` -- *warrant well-known:* j <= succ k => j <= k or j = succ k, for j,k in NN (discreteness of NN).
- `nn-le-imp-neq-succ` -- *warrant well-known:* j <= k => j /= succ k for j,k in NN (succ k > k >= j).

### clear-first-col  *(trust: well-known)*

*trusted code: `crs`* -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

proven modulo:

- `identmat-left-identity` -- *warrant proof:* I_m P = P: the (i,c) entry is sum_j delta_ij P_jc, whose only surviving term
   (j=i) is 1*P_ic = P_ic (matmul-entry + entry-of-identmat + finsum-single-
   support + ring-mul-left-id).  Proof structure worked out in identmat-build.scm.
- `identmat-right-identity` -- *warrant proof:* P I_n = P, the right-handed mirror of identmat-left-identity (only the c=j
   term of sum_j P_ij delta_jc survives).
- `identmat-type` -- *warrant reference:* IDENTMAT(A,n) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A (its entries are ONE/ZERO of A).
- `entry-in-carrier` -- *warrant well-known:* The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
- `nn-not-le-zero-pos` -- *warrant well-known:* 1 <= j => not(j <= 0) for j in NN (0 is least, and j >= 1 > 0).
- `matmul-type` -- *warrant reference:* MAT(m,n,CARR A) x MAT(n,k,CARR A) -> MAT(m,k,CARR A): the tabulated product
   is a matrix whose entries are FINSUMs in the carrier (matof-in-mat +
   finsum-type).
- `matmul-entry` -- *warrant reference:* (P Q)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij}*Q_{jc}, the product summed in A's additive
   group over j in 1..n (entry-of-matof on the MATMUL tabulation).
- `interval-card-in-nn` -- *warrant well-known:* INTERVAL(a,b) is finite, so its cardinality is a natural number.
- `tel-outf-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `tel-tout-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `tel-inf-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `finsum-ring-distrib-right-gen` -- *warrant well-known:* (SUM_z f z) * r = SUM_z (f z * r) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via
   finsum-insert with ring-right-dist; a |-> a*r is an additive-group endomorphism.
- `tel-dist-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `tel-red-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `ring-carrier-closed-mul` -- *warrant proof:* MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).
- `finsum-congruence` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=
   FINSUM(ag,g,S).  Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees
   (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH.  Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on
   the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
- `ter-outf-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `ter-tout-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `ter-gj-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `finsum-ring-distrib-left-gen` -- *warrant well-known:* r*(SUM_z f z) = SUM_z (r * f z) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via
   finsum-insert, r*(SUM_X f + f z0) = r*SUM_X f + r*f z0 by ring-left-dist, then
   the IH.  No commutativity used (a |-> r*a is an additive-group endomorphism).
- `ter-dist-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `ter-red-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `matmul-assoc-summand-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the triple-product summand (c,j) |-> (P_{row,j} Q_{j,c}) R_{c,col} is a function INTERVAL(1,k) x INTERVAL(1,n) -> CARR A: the entries lie in CARR A (entry-in-carrier) and MUL closes on CARR A.
- `finsum-fubini-c` -- *warrant well-known:* finsum-fubini with curried set/finiteness premises (AND-packaged), for fact-friendly forward use.
- `matrix-entry-extensionality` -- *warrant well-known:* Two m-by-n matrices over X with equal entries are equal (tuple/list
   extensionality applied row- and entry-wise).
- `gauge-is-degree` -- *warrant proof:* GAUGE(s) = CHOICE(EUCLIDEAN-GAUGES s) is in FUN(CARR s, NN) and has division-
   with-remainder: IS-EUCLIDEAN-RING(s) makes EUCLIDEAN-GAUGES(s) inhabited
   (euclidean-ring-has-gauge + gauges-mem-build), so the epsilon pick lands in
   it (choice-axiom) and the SEP slices give both conjuncts.  MACHINE-PROVEN in

   (entry-in-carrier gives both factors in CARR A; MUL closes; CARR(RAG)=CARR A).
- `elem-g-rk-vanish` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i=k, j/=k, j/=l.
- `ring-mul-zero-left` -- *warrant well-known:* 0.a = 0: a.0 = a.(0+0) = a.0 + a.0, cancel a.0 in the additive group.  Lang, Algebra II.1.

- `elem-f-cl-off` -- *warrant reference:* F[k,l]_{jc} = 0 for c=l, j/=k.
- `elem-f-cl-at` -- *warrant reference:* F[k,l]_{kc} = 1 for c=l.
- `elem-f-co-off` -- *warrant reference:* F[k,l]_{jc} = 0 for c/=k, c/=l, j/=c.
- `elem-f-co-at` -- *warrant reference:* F[k,l]_{cc} = 1 for c/=k, c/=l.
- `entry-of-identmat` -- *warrant reference:* (IDENTMAT A n)_{ij} = 1 if i=j else 0 (entry-of-matof on the Kronecker delta).
- `elem-f-col-k` -- *warrant reference:* F[k,l]_{ik} = 1 if i=l else 0 (k/=l).
- `elem-f-col-l` -- *warrant reference:* F[k,l]_{il} = 1 if i=k else 0 (k/=l).
- `elem-f-col-other` -- *warrant reference:* F[k,l]_{ic} = 1 if i=c else 0 (c/=k, c/=l).
- `matrix-entry-extensionality` -- *warrant well-known:* Two m-by-n matrices over X with equal entries are equal (tuple/list
   extensionality applied row- and entry-wise).
- `elem-f-rk-off` -- *warrant reference:* F[k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i=k, j/=l.
- `ring-mul-zero-left` -- *warrant well-known:* 0.a = 0: a.0 = a.(0+0) = a.0 + a.0, cancel a.0 in the additive group.  Lang, Algebra II.1.
- `elem-f-rk-at` -- *warrant reference:* F[k,l]_{il} = 1 for i=k.
- `elem-f-rl-off` -- *warrant reference:* F[k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i=l, j/=k.
- `elem-f-rl-at` -- *warrant reference:* F[k,l]_{ik} = 1 for i=l.
- `elem-f-ro-off` -- *warrant reference:* F[k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i/=k, i/=l, j/=i.
- `elem-f-ro-at` -- *warrant reference:* F[k,l]_{ii} = 1 for i/=k, i/=l.
- `tel-outf-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `tel-tout-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `tel-inf-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `finsum-ring-distrib-right-gen` -- *warrant well-known:* (SUM_z f z) * r = SUM_z (f z * r) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via
   finsum-insert with ring-right-dist; a |-> a*r is an additive-group endomorphism.
- `tel-dist-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `tel-red-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `ring-carrier-closed-mul` -- *warrant proof:* MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).
- `finsum-congruence` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=
   FINSUM(ag,g,S).  Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees
   (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH.  Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on
   the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
- `ter-outf-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `ter-tout-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `ter-gj-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `finsum-ring-distrib-left-gen` -- *warrant well-known:* r*(SUM_z f z) = SUM_z (r * f z) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via
   finsum-insert, r*(SUM_X f + f z0) = r*SUM_X f + r*f z0 by ring-left-dist, then
   the IH.  No commutativity used (a |-> r*a is an additive-group endomorphism).
- `ter-dist-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `ter-red-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `matmul-assoc-summand-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the triple-product summand (c,j) |-> (P_{row,j} Q_{j,c}) R_{c,col} is a function INTERVAL(1,k) x INTERVAL(1,n) -> CARR A: the entries lie in CARR A (entry-in-carrier) and MUL closes on CARR A.
- `finsum-fubini-c` -- *warrant well-known:* finsum-fubini with curried set/finiteness premises (AND-packaged), for fact-friendly forward use.
- `nn-not-le-zero-pos` -- *warrant well-known:* 1 <= j => not(j <= 0) for j in NN (0 is least, and j >= 1 > 0).
- `ring-neg-in-carr` -- *warrant well-known:* NEG closes on the carrier (op NEG CARR CARR).
- `elem-g-type` -- *warrant reference:* ELEM-G(A,n,r,k,l) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A when r in CARR A (entries are
   ONE/ZERO/r, all in CARR A; matof-in-mat).
- `elem-g-entry-l-vanish` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{jc} = 0 when c=l and j is neither l (diagonal) nor k (r-slot).
- `finsum-two-support` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(j)=0 for every j in the finite S except j in {i0,i1} (i0/=i1), then
   FINSUM(ag,f,S) = f(i0) * f(i1) in ag.
- `elem-g-entry-l-at-l` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{lc} = 1 when c=l (the diagonal entry of column l).
- `elem-g-entry-l-at-k` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{kc} = r when c=l and k/=l (the r-slot in column l).
- `ras-op` -- *warrant proof:* operation of a ring's additive group is the ring's addition.
- `elem-g-entry-off-l-off-diag` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{jc} = 0 when c/=l and j/=c (off the diagonal, off the r-slot).
- `elem-g-entry-off-l-diag` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{cc} = 1 when c/=l (the diagonal, r-slot inactive).
- `elem-g-col-l` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{il} = 1 if i=l, r if i=k, else 0 (column l: diagonal + the r-slot).
- `elem-g-col-k` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{ik} = 1 if i=k else 0, when k/=l (column k is an identity column).
- `ring-one-in` -- *warrant well-known:* ONE lies in the carrier.
- `ring-add-right-id` -- *warrant well-known:* a + 0 = a (ring-add-left-id + comm).
- `ring-add-right-inv` -- *warrant well-known:* a + (-a) = 0 (ring-add-left-inv + comm).
- `elem-g-col-other` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{ic} = 1 if i=c else 0, when c/=l (any non-l column is identity).
- `ring-neg-neg` -- *warrant well-known:* -(-r) = r in any ring: r's additive inverse's inverse is r (group double-inverse).
- `nn-succ-le-antisym` -- *warrant well-known:* succ a <= b => not(b <= a) for a,b in NN: succ a <= b gives a < b, so b <= a
   would give a < a.  Elementary order on NN.
- `nn-le-succ-cases` -- *warrant well-known:* j <= succ k => j <= k or j = succ k, for j,k in NN (discreteness of NN).
- `nn-le-imp-neq-succ` -- *warrant well-known:* j <= k => j /= succ k for j,k in NN (succ k > k >= j).
- `elem-g-rk-vanish` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i=k, j/=k, j/=l.
- `elem-g-rk-at-k` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{ik} = 1 for i=k (diagonal).
- `elem-g-rk-at-l` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{il} = r for i=k (the E[k,l] entry).
- `elem-g-ro-off` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i/=k, j/=i (identity row).
- `elem-g-ro-at` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{ii} = 1 for i/=k (identity row diagonal).
- `submat-type` -- *warrant reference:* SUBMAT(P,p,q) in MAT(p,q,CARR A) for P in MAT(succ p, succ q, CARR A): each
   block entry is P_{succ i, succ j} in CARR A (entry-in-carrier; succ i in
   [1,succ p] for i in [1,p]), so matof-in-mat applies.
- `border-type` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q) in MAT(succ p, succ q, CARR A): each tabulator value is b
   (in CARR A), ZERO A (in CARR A), or b block entry M_{i-1,j-1} (entry-in-carrier,
   i-1 in [1,p] for i in [2,succ p]); matof-in-mat applies.
- `border-entry-11` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{1,1} = b (i=j=1 branch).
- `border-entry-1j` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{1,j} = 0 for j /= 1 (i=1, j/=1 branch).
- `border-entry-i1` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{i,1} = 0 for i /= 1 (i/=1, j=1 branch).
- `border-entry-block2` -- *warrant reference:* BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{i,j} = M_{i-1,j-1} for i,j /= 1 in the border range (the
   i/=1,j/=1 branch of the def; NN-MINUS = monus predecessor).
- `pred-in-interval` -- *warrant well-known:* i in [2, succ p] => i-1 in [1, p]: the monus predecessor of an index past 1
   lands in the block range.
- `entry-of-submat` -- *warrant reference:* SUBMAT(P,p,q)_{ij} = P_{succ i, succ j} for i in [1,p], j in [1,q]
   (entry-of-matof on the block tabulator, beta-reduced).
- `succ-nn-minus-1` -- *warrant well-known:* succ(i-1) = i for i >= 1 (monus by 1 then succ is the identity on [1,inf)).

   symbols unconnected.  Retire it by constructing + as the recursion it is.
- `nn-not-le-succ-le` -- *warrant well-known:* NN is totally ordered and discrete: not(m<=n) gives n<m, hence succ n <= m.

### compact-countable-product  *(trust: well-known)*

proven modulo:

- `compact-iff-seq-compact-rev` -- *warrant reference:* calculus.pdf Prop 3.12 (1)<=>(3): a metric space is compact iff it is
   sequentially compact.  A cluster point of a sequence is exactly the limit of
   a convergent subsequence (CLUSTER-POINT s f x iff some strictly-mono phi has
   SUBSEQ(f,phi) -> x), so this is compact-iff-cluster-point restated with
   convergent subsequences in place of cluster points.
- `product-metric-default-summable` -- *warrant well-known:* The default weights w(n)=2^-(n+1) are a summable positive sequence
   (geometric, SUM = 1), so PRODUCT-METRIC(ms)=PRODUCT-METRIC-W(ms,2^-(n+1)) is
   a bounded metric (diameter <= 1) realising the product topology.
- `product-is-metric-space` -- *warrant well-known:* PRODUCT-METRIC-W(ms,w) is a metric space.  Nonnegativity, symmetry and the
   zero law are termwise (D_w(x,y)=0 iff every weighted term is 0 iff rho_n=0
   iff x(n)=y(n) for all n iff x=y, as w(n)>0).  The triangle inequality is the
   sum over n of the per-factor bounded triangle inequalities (each rho_n is a
   metric, bdd-metric-is-metric-space), the sums converging by
   product-weighted-summable.
- `seq-compact-countable-product` -- *warrant reference:* Given a sequence seq in the product carrier (= PTS(PRODUCT-METRIC ms), by
   product-metric-carrier), coordinatewise-diagonal-subseq yields a strictly
   monotone delta and L in PRODUCT-CARRIER(ms) with SUBSEQ(seq,delta) converging
   in every coordinate to L(n).  product-convergence-coordinatewise -- at the
   default summable weights (product-metric-default-summable) -- turns
   coordinatewise convergence into CONVERGES-TO(PRODUCT-METRIC ms,
   SUBSEQ(seq,delta), L).  So every product sequence has a convergent
   subsequence: PRODUCT-METRIC(ms) is sequentially compact.
- `compact-iff-seq-compact` -- *warrant reference:* calculus.pdf Prop 3.12 (1)<=>(3): a metric space is compact iff it is
   sequentially compact.  A cluster point of a sequence is exactly the limit of
   a convergent subsequence (CLUSTER-POINT s f x iff some strictly-mono phi has
   SUBSEQ(f,phi) -> x), so this is compact-iff-cluster-point restated with
   convergent subsequences in place of cluster points.

- `rr-le-diff-nonpos` -- *warrant well-known:* u<=v => u-v<=0.
- `rr-prod-nonpos-pos` -- *warrant well-known:* u*v<=0 with v>0 forces u<=0.

- `rr-prod-nonpos-neg` -- *warrant well-known:* u*v<=0 with v<0 forces u>=0.

   <=0 at th (sign preservation under continuity / one-sided limit).
- `continuous-nonneg-left` -- *warrant well-known:* A function continuous at th that is >=0 on a left-neighborhood (aa,th) is
   >=0 at th.
- `min-val-strict-interior` -- *warrant well-known:* c in [a,b], h(a)=h(b), h(c)<h(a) => a<c<b.

- `rr-sub-ne-zero` -- *warrant well-known:* u/=v => u-v/=0.
- `rr-cancel-mul-right` -- *warrant well-known:* u*c=v*c with c/=0 gives u=v (multiply by 1/c).  The right-factor companion of

- `cont-agree-off-pt` -- *warrant well-known:* fa, fb continuous at pt and fa(x)=fb(x) for all x/=pt => fa(pt)=fb(pt).  pt is

- `rr-diff-zero-eq` -- *warrant well-known:* 0=u-v => u=v.

- `rr-lt-from-diff-pos` -- *warrant well-known:* 0 < y-x gives x < y (strict sibling of rr-le-from-diff-nonneg; add x to both

- `matact-type` -- *warrant reference:* P.u is an m-by-q matrix of vectors for P an m-by-n scalar matrix and u an
   n-by-q matrix of vectors: matof-in-mat, whose values are FINSUMs in the
   vector abelian group, typed by finsum-type + mvag-carr.
- `entry-in-carrier` -- *warrant well-known:* The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
- `interval-card-in-nn` -- *warrant well-known:* INTERVAL(a,b) is finite, so its cardinality is a natural number.

   each summand is (matact-summand-type), and the vector abelian group's
   operation closes on its carrier (mvag-carr, mvag-op, module-vadd-type).
- `matact-summand-type` -- *warrant well-known:* j |-> P_{ij} . u_{jc} is a function [1,n] -> VEC md for P:MAT(m,n,CARR(SCAL md))
   and u:MAT(n,q,VEC md): entry-in-carrier types both arguments, module-act-type
   closes the action, and mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) = VEC md.
- `matact-entry` -- *warrant reference:* (P.u)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij} . u_{jc}, summed in the module's vector
   abelian group; the read-off of MATACT's MATOF tabulation via entry-of-matof
   (dimensions m, n, q recovered from SIZE P, SIZE u).
- `matadd-entry` -- *warrant reference:* (P+Q)_{ij} = P_{ij} + Q_{ij}: the read-off of MATADD's MATOF tabulation via
   entry-of-matof + beta, with the dimensions recovered from SIZE P (mat-size).
- `mvag-op` -- *warrant proof:* operation of a module's vector abelian group is the module's vector addition.
- `finsum-congruence` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=
   FINSUM(ag,g,S).  Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees
   (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH.  Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on
   the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
- `finsum-add-ag` -- *warrant well-known:* finsum-add at m = ag as a commutative monoid: induction on |S| via
   finsum-insert-ag, regrouping (a+b)+(c+d)=(a+c)+(b+d) by commutativity and
   associativity of (OPR ag).  No inverses used.
- `mrs-scaled-summand-type` -- *warrant well-known:* z |-> r . (c_{1z} . u_{z1}) is a function [1,n] -> VEC md: the inner action is
   (matact-summand-type), module-act-type closes the outer action, and mvag-carr
   identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) = VEC md.
- `finsum-act-distrib-gen` -- *warrant well-known:* r.(SUM_z f z) = SUM_z (r . f z) in a module: x |-> r.x is an endomorphism of
   the vector abelian group.  Induction on |S| via finsum-insert:
   r.(SUM_X f + f z0) = r.SUM_X f + r.(f z0) by module-act-distrib-vec, and the
   empty case is r.0 = 0, itself r.(0+0) = r.0 + r.0 killed by
   abelian-group-idempotent-is-id on the vector group (the module-zero-act route).
- `matscale-entry` -- *warrant reference:* (r*P)_{ij} = r * P_{ij}: the read-off of MATSCALE's MATOF tabulation via
   entry-of-matof + beta, with the dimensions recovered from SIZE P (mat-size).
- `ring-neg-mul-left` -- *warrant well-known:* (-a)*b = -(a*b): a*b + (-a)*b = (a + -a)*b = 0*b = 0 (ring-right-dist,
   ring-add-left-inv, ring-mul-zero-left), so (-a)*b is the inverse of a*b.
- `ring-carrier-closed-mul` -- *warrant proof:* MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).
- `ring-add-right-inv` -- *warrant well-known:* a + (-a) = 0 (ring-add-left-inv + comm).
- `nn-one-in` -- *warrant well-known:* 1 = succ 0 in NN.
- `nn-le-succ` -- *warrant well-known:* k <= succ k on NN.
- `block-type` -- *warrant reference:* The leading k-by-l block of an m-by-n matrix over X is a k-by-l matrix over X
   (matof-in-mat; every value ENTRY P i j with i<=k<=m, j<=l<=n lies in X).

   type's argument, restricted -- entry-in-carrier still types both arguments for
   j in [1,k] subset [1,n] (interval [1,k] subset [1,n] since k <= n).
- `finsum-interval-peel` -- *warrant well-known:* FINSUM over [1,succ n] = (FINSUM over [1,n]) . f(succ n): finsum-insert-ag at
   X = [1,n], k = succ n, using [1,succ n] = [1,n] u {succ n} (interval-mem-intro
   / interval-hi + nn-le-succ-cases) and succ n not in [1,n] (interval-hi would
   give succ n <= n).  Standard finite-sum back-peel, the twin of
   finsum-interval-shift.
- `entry-of-block` -- *warrant reference:* BLOCK(P,k,l)_{ij} = P_{ij} for i in [1,k], j in [1,l] (entry-of-matof).
- `finsum-type` -- *warrant informal:* Closure of the finite sum on the carrier.  Induction on card(S): the fold
   seeds at (IDEN ag), in (CARR ag) by the identity law, and each step applies
   (OPR ag), which closes on (CARR ag) by the group's binary-operation typing.
   No inverses used -- the same argument as finsum-comm-monoid-type.
- `mvag-carr` -- *warrant proof:* carrier of a module's vector abelian group is the module's vector set.
- `abelian-group-idempotent-is-id` -- **NO WARRANT**
- `abelian-group-right-id` -- *warrant well-known:* a * e = e * a = a, by abelian-group-opr-comm then group-left-id (via
   abelian-group-is-group).
- `abelian-group-assoc` -- *warrant well-known:* (a*b)*c = a*(b*c): group-assoc via abelian-group-is-group.

### diagonalization  *(trust: well-known)*

*trusted code: `arith`* -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

proven modulo:

- `inf-subset-nn-unbounded` -- *warrant well-known:* An infinite subset T of NN is unbounded: if every y in T satisfied y <= u,
   then T subset {0,...,u} = ORD-SEGMENT(succ u), a finite set, forcing CARD T
   in NN and contradicting T in INF-SUBSETS(NN).  Hence some y in T has u < y;
   and y in NN since T subset NN.  The contrapositive 'bounded subset of NN is
   finite'.
- `dc-on-nn-pred` -- *warrant reference:* Set-valued-step form of dc-on-nn (same file).  Derivable from primitive
   recursion on NN + global Hilbert choice: g(k,u):=CHOICE{y in X:y in nxt
   (k,u)} is defined by the totality hypothesis, then recurse f(0):=a,
   f(succ k):=g(k,f k); f:NN->X by induction.  No relation-set encoding --
   nxt(k,u) is the successor SET, supplied as a VNB-LAMBDA, so callers avoid
   the (LIST k u y)-in-R comprehension/sethood/NTH plumbing (O1 of
   archive/calculus-pre-rename/block-family-rederive.scm).  Accepted asserted during library-build.
- `nn-step-strictly-mono` -- *warrant well-known:* g:NN->NN with g(k) < g(succ k) for all k is strictly monotone.  For m < n,
   write n reached from m by succ-steps and chain g(m) < g(m+1) < ... < g(n)
   by transitivity of < (rr-lt-trans); formally NN-induction on n using
   m < succ n  iff  m <= n.  STRICTLY-MONO-NN also wants g in FUN(NN,NN),
   supplied as the hypothesis.  Textbook.
- `nn-one-le-succ` -- *warrant well-known:* 1 <= succ n for every n in NN.
- `nn-not-le-zero-pos` -- *warrant well-known:* 1 <= j => not(j <= 0) for j in NN (0 is least, and j >= 1 > 0).
- `nn-le-succ-cases` -- *warrant well-known:* j <= succ k => j <= k or j = succ k, for j,k in NN (discreteness of NN).

### diff-implies-continuous  *(trust: well-known)*

*trusted code: `crs`* -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

proven modulo:

- `const-continuous-at` -- *warrant well-known:* Constant map: for any eps>0 any delta>0 works, since d(c,c)=0<=eps.
- `identity-continuous-at` -- *warrant well-known:* Identity map: delta=eps works, since d(x,a)=|x-a|<=eps whenever |x-a|<=eps.
- `sub-continuous-at` -- *warrant well-known:* Difference of continuous is continuous: (g-h)(x) = g(x) + (-1)*h(x); the eps/2
   split for sum-continuous-at, negation being an isometry of RR.
- `product-continuous-at` -- *warrant well-known:* Product of continuous: g is bounded near a (continuity), and
   |gh(x)-gh(a)| <= |g(x)||h(x)-h(a)| + |h(a)||g(x)-g(a)|; choose deltas making
   each summand < eps/2.
- `sum-continuous-at` -- *warrant well-known:* Sum of continuous: given eps, take delta = min of the eps/2-deltas for g and
   h; the triangle inequality gives |(g+h)(x)-(g+h)(a)| <= eps.
- `cont-transfer-ptwise-eq` -- *warrant well-known:* f = g pointwise and g continuous at a => f continuous at a: continuity reads
   only the values, and d(f(x),f(a)) = d(g(x),g(a)) at every x, so the same delta
   works.

   f(w-z) <= M||w-z|| <= M(||z+v||+||w+v||), so sup_z(-f(z)-M||z+v||) <=
   inf_w(-f(w)+M||w+v||); RR order-completeness gives a separating alpha, whence
   |f(y)+r.alpha| <= M||y+r.v|| for all y in s, r in RR.
- `hb-extend-construct` -- *warrant reference:* Extending a linear functional one dimension: with v notin s every element of
   s + RR.v is uniquely y + r.v, so g(y+r.v) = f(y) + r.alpha is a well-defined
   linear functional on s + RR.v extending f, for any scalar alpha.  Standard
   linear algebra; alpha is fixed only by the bound (hb-gap).

   r in RR. w = y + r.v); a member iff in VEC(m) and it so decomposes.

   contains 0, so it is a submodule.

- `vspace-vec-is-set` -- *warrant reference:* The carrier VEC(m) of a vector space (module) is a set.
- `good-sub-submodule` -- *warrant reference:* GOOD-SUB unfolds to NPE, whose first conjunct is IS-SUBMODULE m t.
- `good-sub-self` -- *warrant reference:* f is a norm-preserving extension of itself to s (bound = ||f||_s), so s is reachable.
- `good-sub-in-power` -- *warrant reference:* A reachable t is a submodule, hence a subset of VEC(m), hence in its powerset.
- `dc-on-nn-pred` -- *warrant reference:* Set-valued-step form of dc-on-nn (same file).  Derivable from primitive
   recursion on NN + global Hilbert choice: g(k,u):=CHOICE{y in X:y in nxt
   (k,u)} is defined by the totality hypothesis, then recurse f(0):=a,
   f(succ k):=g(k,f k); f:NN->X by induction.  No relation-set encoding --
   nxt(k,u) is the successor SET, supplied as a VNB-LAMBDA, so callers avoid
   the (LIST k u y)-in-R comprehension/sethood/NTH plumbing (O1 of
   archive/calculus-pre-rename/block-family-rederive.scm).  Accepted asserted during library-build.
- `fun-codomain-superset` -- *warrant well-known:* A function into B is a function into any superset C (fun-codomain-iff + subset-mem).
- `nn-le-succ` -- *warrant well-known:* k <= succ k on NN.

### interior-max-deriv-zero  *(trust: none)*

proven modulo:

- `nary-minus-2` -- **NO WARRANT**
- `binneg-in-fun-rr` -- *warrant well-known:* Closure of a primitive numeric domain under its arithmetic operation; standard.
- `binplus-in-fun-rr` -- *warrant well-known:* Closure of a primitive numeric domain under its arithmetic operation; standard.
- `rr-lt-trans` -- *warrant well-known:* Strict order is transitive: from x<y<z, x<=z by rr-leq-transitive and x=/=z
   (else y would be both > and < x).
- `rr-lt-implies-le` -- *warrant well-known:* x < y is (x <= y and x =/= y) by definition, so in particular x <= y.
- `rr-lt-diff-pos` -- *warrant well-known:* u<v => 0<v-u.
- `ccint-membership` -- *warrant proof:* Separation: x in CCINT(a,b) = SEP(x in RR | a<=x and x<=b) iff x in RR and
   a<=x and x<=b, by the SEP membership kernel rule.
- `rr-le-diff-nonpos` -- *warrant well-known:* u<=v => u-v<=0.
- `rr-prod-nonpos-pos` -- *warrant well-known:* u*v<=0 with v>0 forces u<=0.
- `rr-lt-diff-neg` -- *warrant well-known:* u<v => u-v<0.
- `rr-prod-nonpos-neg` -- *warrant well-known:* u*v<=0 with v<0 forces u>=0.
- `continuous-nonpos-right` -- *warrant well-known:* A function continuous at th that is <=0 on a right-neighborhood (th,bb) is
   <=0 at th (sign preservation under continuity / one-sided limit).
- `continuous-nonneg-left` -- *warrant well-known:* A function continuous at th that is >=0 on a left-neighborhood (aa,th) is
   >=0 at th.

   (the right-to-left direction of interval-membership's SEP iff).
- `zeromat-type` -- *warrant reference:* the all-zero m-by-n matrix is m-by-n over CARR A.
- `entry-of-zeromat` -- *warrant reference:* (ZEROMAT A m n)_{ij} = 0 (entry-of-matof on the constant tabulation).
- `interval-card-in-nn` -- *warrant well-known:* INTERVAL(a,b) is finite, so its cardinality is a natural number.
- `matact-summand-type` -- *warrant well-known:* j |-> P_{ij} . u_{jc} is a function [1,n] -> VEC md for P:MAT(m,n,CARR(SCAL md))
   and u:MAT(n,q,VEC md): entry-in-carrier types both arguments, module-act-type
   closes the action, and mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) = VEC md.
- `matact-entry` -- *warrant reference:* (P.u)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij} . u_{jc}, summed in the module's vector
   abelian group; the read-off of MATACT's MATOF tabulation via entry-of-matof
   (dimensions m, n, q recovered from SIZE P, SIZE u).
- `mvag-id` -- *warrant proof:* identity of a module's vector abelian group is the module's zero vector.
- `entry-in-carrier` -- *warrant well-known:* The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
- `abelian-group-idempotent-is-id` -- **NO WARRANT**
- `finsum-all-id` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(z) = IDEN(ag) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S) =
   IDEN(ag).  Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term is IDEN and the
   rest is IDEN by the IH, so the sum is IDEN*IDEN = IDEN; the base case is finsum-empty.
- `ring-add-closed` -- *warrant well-known:* ADD closes on the carrier (curried form).
- `matadd-type` -- *warrant reference:* entrywise sum of m-by-n matrices is m-by-n.
- `matadd-entry` -- *warrant reference:* (P+Q)_{ij} = P_{ij} + Q_{ij}: the read-off of MATADD's MATOF tabulation via
   entry-of-matof + beta, with the dimensions recovered from SIZE P (mat-size).
- `mra-combined-summand-type` -- *warrant well-known:* j |-> (c1_{1j}.u_{j1}) (+) (c2_{1j}.u_{j1}) is a function [1,n] -> VEC md:
   each summand is (matact-summand-type), and the vector abelian group's
   operation closes on its carrier (mvag-carr, mvag-op, module-vadd-type).
- `mvag-op` -- *warrant proof:* operation of a module's vector abelian group is the module's vector addition.
- `finsum-congruence` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=
   FINSUM(ag,g,S).  Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees
   (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH.  Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on
   the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
- `finsum-add-ag` -- *warrant well-known:* finsum-add at m = ag as a commutative monoid: induction on |S| via
   finsum-insert-ag, regrouping (a+b)+(c+d)=(a+c)+(b+d) by commutativity and
   associativity of (OPR ag).  No inverses used.
- `matscale-type` -- *warrant reference:* entrywise scalar multiple of an m-by-n matrix is m-by-n (MUL closes on CARR A).
- `matscale-entry` -- *warrant reference:* (r*P)_{ij} = r * P_{ij}: the read-off of MATSCALE's MATOF tabulation via
   entry-of-matof + beta, with the dimensions recovered from SIZE P (mat-size).
- `ring-neg-mul-left` -- *warrant well-known:* (-a)*b = -(a*b): a*b + (-a)*b = (a + -a)*b = 0*b = 0 (ring-right-dist,
   ring-add-left-inv, ring-mul-zero-left), so (-a)*b is the inverse of a*b.
- `mrs-scaled-summand-type` -- *warrant well-known:* z |-> r . (c_{1z} . u_{z1}) is a function [1,n] -> VEC md: the inner action is
   (matact-summand-type), module-act-type closes the outer action, and mvag-carr
   identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) = VEC md.
- `finsum-act-distrib-gen` -- *warrant well-known:* r.(SUM_z f z) = SUM_z (r . f z) in a module: x |-> r.x is an endomorphism of
   the vector abelian group.  Induction on |S| via finsum-insert:
   r.(SUM_X f + f z0) = r.SUM_X f + r.(f z0) by module-act-distrib-vec, and the
   empty case is r.0 = 0, itself r.(0+0) = r.0 + r.0 killed by
   abelian-group-idempotent-is-id on the vector group (the module-zero-act route).
- `ring-carrier-closed-mul` -- *warrant proof:* MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).

- `ring-carr-in-set` -- *warrant proof:* carrier of a ring is a set (IS-RING typing conjunct).
- `mat-is-set` -- *warrant reference:* MAT(m,n,X) is a SEP over the set MATRIX(X), hence a set.
- `mat-ring-add` -- *warrant reference:* slot 2 of the MAT-RING tuple (entrywise sum).
- `mat-ring-add-fun` -- *warrant reference:* curried entrywise sum is a function MAT x MAT -> MAT (2-binder).
- `mat-ring-mul` -- *warrant reference:* slot 3 of the MAT-RING tuple (matrix product).
- `mat-ring-mul-fun` -- *warrant reference:* curried product is a function MAT x MAT -> MAT (2-binder).
- `mat-ring-neg` -- *warrant reference:* slot 4 of the MAT-RING tuple (entrywise negation).
- `matneg-type` -- *warrant reference:* entrywise negation of an m-by-n matrix is m-by-n.
- `mat-ring-zero` -- *warrant reference:* slot 5 of the MAT-RING tuple (all-zero matrix).
- `zeromat-type` -- *warrant reference:* the all-zero m-by-n matrix is m-by-n over CARR A.
- `mat-ring-one` -- *warrant reference:* slot 6 of the MAT-RING tuple (identity matrix).
- `identmat-type` -- *warrant reference:* IDENTMAT(A,n) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A (its entries are ONE/ZERO of A).
- `matadd-type` -- *warrant reference:* entrywise sum of m-by-n matrices is m-by-n.
- `matadd-assoc` -- *warrant reference:* matrix addition is associative (entrywise, ring-add-assoc).
- `matadd-comm` -- *warrant reference:* matrix addition is commutative (entrywise, ring-add-comm).
- `matadd-zero-left` -- *warrant reference:* ZEROMAT is a left identity for MATADD (entrywise 0+x=x).
- `matadd-zero-right` -- *warrant reference:* ZEROMAT is a right identity for MATADD (matadd-comm + zero-left).
- `matadd-neg-left` -- *warrant reference:* MATNEG is a left inverse for MATADD (entrywise (-x)+x=0).
- `matadd-neg-right` -- *warrant reference:* MATNEG is a right inverse for MATADD (matadd-comm + neg-left).
- `matmul-type` -- *warrant reference:* MAT(m,n,CARR A) x MAT(n,k,CARR A) -> MAT(m,k,CARR A): the tabulated product
   is a matrix whose entries are FINSUMs in the carrier (matof-in-mat +
   finsum-type).
- `matmul-entry` -- *warrant reference:* (P Q)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij}*Q_{jc}, the product summed in A's additive
   group over j in 1..n (entry-of-matof on the MATMUL tabulation).
- `interval-card-in-nn` -- *warrant well-known:* INTERVAL(a,b) is finite, so its cardinality is a natural number.
- `tel-outf-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `tel-tout-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `tel-inf-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `entry-in-carrier` -- *warrant well-known:* The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
- `finsum-ring-distrib-right-gen` -- *warrant well-known:* (SUM_z f z) * r = SUM_z (f z * r) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via
   finsum-insert with ring-right-dist; a |-> a*r is an additive-group endomorphism.
- `tel-dist-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `tel-red-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `ring-carrier-closed-mul` -- *warrant proof:* MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).
- `finsum-congruence` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=
   FINSUM(ag,g,S).  Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees
   (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH.  Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on
   the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
- `ter-outf-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `ter-tout-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `ter-gj-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `finsum-ring-distrib-left-gen` -- *warrant well-known:* r*(SUM_z f z) = SUM_z (r * f z) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via
   finsum-insert, r*(SUM_X f + f z0) = r*SUM_X f + r*f z0 by ring-left-dist, then
   the IH.  No commutativity used (a |-> r*a is an additive-group endomorphism).
- `ter-dist-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `ter-red-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
- `matmul-assoc-summand-type` -- *warrant well-known:* the triple-product summand (c,j) |-> (P_{row,j} Q_{j,c}) R_{c,col} is a function INTERVAL(1,k) x INTERVAL(1,n) -> CARR A: the entries lie in CARR A (entry-in-carrier) and MUL closes on CARR A.
- `finsum-fubini-c` -- *warrant well-known:* finsum-fubini with curried set/finiteness premises (AND-packaged), for fact-friendly forward use.
- `matrix-entry-extensionality` -- *warrant well-known:* Two m-by-n matrices over X with equal entries are equal (tuple/list
   extensionality applied row- and entry-wise).
- `identmat-left-identity` -- *warrant proof:* I_m P = P: the (i,c) entry is sum_j delta_ij P_jc, whose only surviving term
   (j=i) is 1*P_ic = P_ic (matmul-entry + entry-of-identmat + finsum-single-
   support + ring-mul-left-id).  Proof structure worked out in identmat-build.scm.
- `identmat-right-identity` -- *warrant proof:* P I_n = P, the right-handed mirror of identmat-left-identity (only the c=j
   term of sum_j P_ij delta_jc survives).
- `matmul-left-dist` -- *warrant reference:* P(Q+R) = PQ + PR: distribute inside the sum termwise (finsum-additive + ring-left-dist).
- `matmul-right-dist` -- *warrant reference:* (P+Q)R = PR + QR (finsum-additive + ring-right-dist).

### matact-empty-vzero  *(trust: well-known)*

proven modulo:

- `one-in-interval-1` -- *warrant proof:* 1 in INTERVAL(1,1): 1 in NN and 1<=1<=1.  The column index of a column vector.

   abelian group; the read-off of MATACT's MATOF tabulation via entry-of-matof
   (dimensions m, n, q recovered from SIZE P, SIZE u).
- `interval-1-0-empty` -- *warrant well-known:* [1,0] is empty: i in [1,0] gives 1 <= i and i <= 0, hence 1 <= 0, false in NN.
- `finsum-empty` -- *warrant informal:* Mechanically proven before the 2026-05-27 PSS promotion: mac FINSUM exposes
   SUM-AG over CARD(EMPTY-SET), card-empty rewrites that to 0, and sum-ag-zero
   collapses the empty sum to the identity.  Script archived at
   archive/proven-theorems-archive.scm (prove-and-install! 'finsum-empty).  The
   archive predates the E -> IDEN accessor rename and the ==-sweep, so it is a
   record of the argument rather than a runnable script -- which is why this is
   `informal' and not `proof'.
- `mvag-id` -- *warrant proof:* identity of a module's vector abelian group is the module's zero vector.

   X = [1,n], k = succ n, using [1,succ n] = [1,n] u {succ n} (interval-mem-intro
   / interval-hi + nn-le-succ-cases) and succ n not in [1,n] (interval-hi would
   give succ n <= n).  Standard finite-sum back-peel, the twin of
   finsum-interval-shift.
- `mvag-op` -- *warrant proof:* operation of a module's vector abelian group is the module's vector addition.
- `nn-one-le-succ` -- *warrant well-known:* 1 <= succ n for every n in NN.
- `interval-mem-intro` -- *warrant proof:* Converse of interval-lo/interval-hi: i in NN with a<=i<=b lies in INTERVAL(a,b)
   (the right-to-left direction of interval-membership's SEP iff).
- `entry-of-block` -- *warrant reference:* BLOCK(P,k,l)_{ij} = P_{ij} for i in [1,k], j in [1,l] (entry-of-matof).
- `entry-in-carrier` -- *warrant well-known:* The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
- `finsum-congruence` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=

   (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH.  Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on
   the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
- `finsum-type` -- *warrant informal:* Closure of the finite sum on the carrier.  Induction on card(S): the fold
   seeds at (IDEN ag), in (CARR ag) by the identity law, and each step applies
   (OPR ag), which closes on (CARR ag) by the group's binary-operation typing.
   No inverses used -- the same argument as finsum-comm-monoid-type.
- `mvag-carr` -- *warrant proof:* carrier of a module's vector abelian group is the module's vector set.

### metric-top-functorial  *(trust: well-known)*

proven modulo:

- `metric-hom-is-continuous` -- *warrant well-known:* An isometry preserves distance, so it is uniformly continuous (delta = eps) and hence continuous.
- `empty-is-open` -- *warrant proof:* EMPTY-SET subset PTS(s) holds vacuously and the point-quantifier in IS-OPEN ranges over no points; unfold IS-OPEN and discharge both conjuncts.
- `carrier-is-open` -- *warrant proof:* SUBSET(PTS(s),PTS(s)) is reflexivity; at each point pick any r > 0, and BALL(s,y,r) subset PTS(s) (a SEP over PTS(s), via the kernel sep rule).
- `inter-of-opens-open` -- *warrant proof:* At a point y in U cap W, openness gives balls of radii r_U, r_W inside U, W respectively; the ball of radius min(r_U,r_W) lies in both, hence in the intersection.
- `union-of-opens-open` -- *warrant proof:* A point y of the union lies in some (g i) with i in A; the ball witnessing openness of (g i) at y is contained in (g i), hence in the union. Subset-of-carrier is inherited termwise.
- `continuous-implies-open-preimage` -- *warrant proof:* MACHINE-PROVEN in archive/calculus-pre-rename/prop-3-15-proof.scm (qed; proven modulo {rr-pos-shrink, ball-mem-from-le, ball-membership, continuous-is-continuous-at}; the PREIMAGE-subset-PTS(s) conjunct goes straight through the kernel SEP rule sep-me).  Sketch: let a in PREIMAGE(s,f,V), so f(a) in V open: some eps-ball B(t,f(a),eps) subset V.  Shrink eps to half<eps (rr-pos-shrink); continuity at a for half gives delta>0 with d(s)(a,z)<=delta => d(t)(f a,f z)<=half<eps, so f(z) in B(t,f(a),eps) subset V (ball-mem-from-le bridges the non-strict bound to strict ball membership).  Hence B(s,a,delta) subset PREIMAGE(s,f,V): the preimage is open.

- `bdd-metric-is-bounded-metric-space` -- *warrant well-known:* BDD-METRIC(s) is a bounded metric space: a metric space by
   bdd-metric-is-metric-space, and 1 in RR bounds every distance since
   d/(1+d) < 1 <= 1 (bdd-metric-bounded).
- `bdd-metric-preserves-metric-top` -- *warrant well-known:* METRIC-TOP(BDD-METRIC s) = METRIC-TOP(s): d and d/(1+d) are topologically
   equivalent.  The identity map is bicontinuous (bdd-metric-id-bicontinuous), so
   applying continuous-implies-open-preimage to the identity (whose preimage of a
   set is that set) in both directions gives IS-OPEN(s,U) <=> IS-OPEN(BDD-METRIC s, U);
   the carriers agree (bdd-metric-carrier), so the two [carrier, opens] tuples are
   equal.
- `empty-is-open` -- *warrant proof:* EMPTY-SET subset PTS(s) holds vacuously and the point-quantifier in IS-OPEN ranges over no points; unfold IS-OPEN and discharge both conjuncts.
- `carrier-is-open` -- *warrant proof:* SUBSET(PTS(s),PTS(s)) is reflexivity; at each point pick any r > 0, and BALL(s,y,r) subset PTS(s) (a SEP over PTS(s), via the kernel sep rule).
- `inter-of-opens-open` -- *warrant proof:* At a point y in U cap W, openness gives balls of radii r_U, r_W inside U, W respectively; the ball of radius min(r_U,r_W) lies in both, hence in the intersection.
- `union-of-opens-open` -- *warrant proof:* A point y of the union lies in some (g i) with i in A; the ball witnessing openness of (g i) at y is contained in (g i), hence in the union. Subset-of-carrier is inherited termwise.

### mvt  *(trust: none)*

*trusted code: `crs`* -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

proven modulo:

- `nary-times-2` -- **NO WARRANT**
- `nary-minus-2` -- **NO WARRANT**
- `binneg-in-fun-rr` -- *warrant well-known:* Closure of a primitive numeric domain under its arithmetic operation; standard.
- `binplus-in-fun-rr` -- *warrant well-known:* Closure of a primitive numeric domain under its arithmetic operation; standard.
- `bintimes-in-fun-rr` -- *warrant well-known:* Closure of a primitive numeric domain under its arithmetic operation; standard.
- `mvt-aux-cont` -- *warrant reference:* h is a sum/product of f, constants and the identity, hence continuous where f is.
- `mvt-aux-diff` -- *warrant reference:* h=(b-a)f-(f(b)-f(a))id is a linear combination of f and identity; its
   derivative (b-a)L-(f(b)-f(a)) follows from deriv-product/sum/const/identity.
- `rr-lt-implies-le` -- *warrant well-known:* x < y is (x <= y and x =/= y) by definition, so in particular x <= y.
- `extreme-value-max` -- *warrant reference:* Extreme Value Theorem (calculus.pdf Ch 2.5, used in Rolle's lemma): a
   continuous f on the compact [a,b] attains its supremum.  Sequentially: a
   maximizing sequence in [a,b] has a convergent subsequence (seq-compactness of
   [a,b]); its limit c is in [a,b] and f(c) is the max by sequential continuity.
- `extreme-value-min` -- *warrant reference:* EVT (min form): apply extreme-value-max to -f; the argmax of -f is the argmin
   of f.
- `ccint-membership` -- *warrant proof:* Separation: x in CCINT(a,b) = SEP(x in RR | a<=x and x<=b) iff x in RR and
   a<=x and x<=b, by the SEP membership kernel rule.
- `rr-le-cases` -- *warrant well-known:* u<=v => u<v or u=v.
- `max-val-strict-interior` -- *warrant well-known:* c in [a,b], h(a)=h(b), h(a)<h(c) => a<c<b (a strict max above equal endpoints is interior).
- `rr-lt-trans` -- *warrant well-known:* Strict order is transitive: from x<y<z, x<=z by rr-leq-transitive and x=/=z
   (else y would be both > and < x).
- `rr-lt-diff-pos` -- *warrant well-known:* u<v => 0<v-u.
- `rr-le-diff-nonpos` -- *warrant well-known:* u<=v => u-v<=0.
- `rr-prod-nonpos-pos` -- *warrant well-known:* u*v<=0 with v>0 forces u<=0.
- `rr-lt-diff-neg` -- *warrant well-known:* u<v => u-v<0.
- `rr-prod-nonpos-neg` -- *warrant well-known:* u*v<=0 with v<0 forces u>=0.
- `continuous-nonpos-right` -- *warrant well-known:* A function continuous at th that is <=0 on a right-neighborhood (th,bb) is
   <=0 at th (sign preservation under continuity / one-sided limit).
- `continuous-nonneg-left` -- *warrant well-known:* A function continuous at th that is >=0 on a left-neighborhood (aa,th) is
   >=0 at th.
- `min-val-strict-interior` -- *warrant well-known:* c in [a,b], h(a)=h(b), h(c)<h(a) => a<c<b.
- `deriv-neg` -- *warrant reference:* Derivative of -f is -f': f(x)-f(a)=phi(x)(x-a) gives (-f)(x)-(-f)(a) =
   (-phi)(x)(x-a), with -phi continuous at a and value -L.
- `nary-neg-1` -- **NO WARRANT**
- `rr-le-neg` -- *warrant well-known:* u<=v => -v<=-u.
- `rr-neg-eq-zero` -- *warrant well-known:* -u=0 => u=0.
- `rr-midpoint-between` -- *warrant well-known:* a<b => exists m with a<m<b.
- `diff-value-real` -- *warrant informal:* IS-DIFF-AT's definition includes (IN L RR) as a conjunct.
- `rr-sub-ne-zero` -- *warrant well-known:* u/=v => u-v/=0.
- `rr-cancel-mul-right` -- *warrant well-known:* u*c=v*c with c/=0 gives u=v (multiply by 1/c).  The right-factor companion of
   rr-cancel-mul-left (taylor-proof.scm).
- `cont-agree-off-pt` -- *warrant well-known:* fa, fb continuous at pt and fa(x)=fb(x) for all x/=pt => fa(pt)=fb(pt).  pt is
   a limit point of RR (no isolated points), so both values are the common limit
   of the punctured values; take x -> pt.
- `rr-diff-zero-eq` -- *warrant well-known:* 0=u-v => u=v.

### no-injection-seg-1-into-seg-0  *(trust: informal)*

proven modulo:

- `ord-segment-nn-succ` -- *warrant informal:* Mechanically proven before the 2026-05-27 PSS promotion; script archived at
   archive/proven-theorems-archive.scm (prove-and-install! 'ord-segment-nn-succ).
   The NN-flavoured successor characterisation: ORD-SEGMENT(succ n) is

   transitivity gives p<=b*n.
- `line-functional-exists` -- *warrant reference:* On the one-dimensional subspace RR.v the map r.v |-> r.||v|| is a bounded
   linear functional of operator norm <= 1 (=1 for v/=0) taking the value ||v||
   at v; for v=0 the zero functional serves.
- `line-is-submodule` -- *warrant reference:* RR.v is the image of the scalar action, closed under addition, negation and
   the action, and contains 0 = 0.v; so it is a submodule.
- `vspace-vec-is-set` -- *warrant reference:* The carrier VEC(m) of a vector space (module) is a set.
- `good-sub-submodule` -- *warrant reference:* GOOD-SUB unfolds to NPE, whose first conjunct is IS-SUBMODULE m t.
- `good-sub-self` -- *warrant reference:* f is a norm-preserving extension of itself to s (bound = ||f||_s), so s is reachable.
- `good-sub-in-power` -- *warrant reference:* A reachable t is a submodule, hence a subset of VEC(m), hence in its powerset.
- `dc-on-nn-pred` -- *warrant reference:* Set-valued-step form of dc-on-nn (same file).  Derivable from primitive
   recursion on NN + global Hilbert choice: g(k,u):=CHOICE{y in X:y in nxt
   (k,u)} is defined by the totality hypothesis, then recurse f(0):=a,
   f(succ k):=g(k,f k); f:NN->X by induction.  No relation-set encoding --
   nxt(k,u) is the successor SET, supplied as a VNB-LAMBDA, so callers avoid
   the (LIST k u y)-in-R comprehension/sethood/NTH plumbing (O1 of
   archive/calculus-pre-rename/block-family-rederive.scm).  Accepted asserted during library-build.
- `fun-codomain-superset` -- *warrant well-known:* A function into B is a function into any superset C (fun-codomain-iff + subset-mem).
- `nn-le-succ` -- *warrant well-known:* k <= succ k on NN.
- `submodule-subset` -- *warrant reference:* A submodule is by definition a subset of the vectors.
- `proper-subset-witness` -- *warrant well-known:* If a subset b but a != b then by extensionality some x in b is not in a.
- `dual-norm-on-nonneg` -- *warrant reference:* DUAL-NORM-ON is defined by IOTA over c in RR with 0<=c, so for a bounded
   functional it is a nonnegative real.
- `hb-gap` -- *warrant reference:* Hahn-Banach gap (real, one dimension): with M = ||f||_s, for z,w in s
   f(w-z) <= M||w-z|| <= M(||z+v||+||w+v||), so sup_z(-f(z)-M||z+v||) <=
   inf_w(-f(w)+M||w+v||); RR order-completeness gives a separating alpha, whence
   |f(y)+r.alpha| <= M||y+r.v|| for all y in s, r in RR.
- `hb-extend-construct` -- *warrant reference:* Extending a linear functional one dimension: with v notin s every element of
   s + RR.v is uniquely y + r.v, so g(y+r.v) = f(y) + r.alpha is a well-defined
   linear functional on s + RR.v extending f, for any scalar alpha.  Standard
   linear algebra; alpha is fixed only by the bound (hb-gap).
- `span-add-one-membership` -- *warrant proof:* Separation membership: SPAN-ADD-ONE(m,s,v) = SEP(w in VEC(m) | exists y in s,
   r in RR. w = y + r.v); a member iff in VEC(m) and it so decomposes.
- `span-add-one-submodule` -- *warrant reference:* t + RR.v is closed under addition, negation and the scalar action and
   contains 0, so it is a submodule.
- `span-add-one-superset` -- *warrant reference:* Every y in t equals y + 0.v, so t is contained in t + RR.v.
- `extends-on-trans` -- *warrant reference:* If g2=g1 on t, g1=f on s, and s subset t, then g2=f on s.
- `linfun-app-abs-real` -- *warrant reference:* A linear functional on t maps into RR, so |g(w)| is a real for w in t.
- `dual-norm-on-le-bound` -- *warrant reference:* DUAL-NORM-ON(m,s,g) is the least c>=0 bounding |g(w)| by c*||w|| on s
   (IOTA least-upper-bound), hence <= any such bound c.
- `span-add-one-has-v` -- *warrant reference:* v = 0 + 1.v with 0 in t, so v is in t + RR.v.
- `vec-is-set` -- *warrant reference:* VEC(m) in SET, a defining clause of IS-NORMED-VECTOR-SPACE (definedness).
- `linfun-on-vec-is-linfun` -- *warrant reference:* IS-LINEAR-FUNCTIONAL-ON m (VEC m) f and IS-LINEAR-FUNCTIONAL m f unfold to the
   same conjunction (f in FUN(VEC m,RR), additive, homogeneous over VEC m).
- `dual-norm-le-bound` -- *warrant reference:* DUAL-NORM(m,f) is the least c>=0 bounding |f(w)| by c*||w|| on VEC(m) (IOTA
   least-upper-bound), hence <= any such bound c.
- `line-has-v` -- *warrant reference:* v = 1.v, so v is in RR.v = LINE(m,v).
- `abs-nonneg-le` -- *warrant well-known:* For a real a >= 0, |a| = a, so |a| <= c gives a <= c.

### pigeonhole-segments  *(trust: well-known)*

*trusted code: `arith`, `ineq`* -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

proven modulo:

- `ord-segment-nn-succ` -- *warrant informal:* Mechanically proven before the 2026-05-27 PSS promotion; script archived at
   archive/proven-theorems-archive.scm (prove-and-install! 'ord-segment-nn-succ).
   The NN-flavoured successor characterisation: ORD-SEGMENT(succ n) is
   ORD-SEGMENT(n) with n adjoined, which is the ordinal successor read at NN.
   Archive predates the E -> IDEN rename and the ==-sweep.
- `nn-le-succ-cases` -- *warrant well-known:* j <= succ k => j <= k or j = succ k, for j,k in NN (discreteness of NN).
- `nn-le-succ` -- *warrant well-known:* k <= succ k on NN.
- `co-le-trans` -- *warrant well-known:* a<=b then b<=c gives a<=c.
- `nn-le-imp-neq-succ` -- *warrant well-known:* j <= k => j /= succ k for j,k in NN (succ k > k >= j).
- `ord-segment-nn-subset` -- *warrant informal:* Mechanically proven before the 2026-05-27 PSS promotion; script archived at
   archive/proven-theorems-archive.scm (prove-and-install! 'ord-segment-nn-subset).
   A member of ORD-SEGMENT(m) for m in NN is an ordinal below a natural, hence
   itself a natural, by NN-induction on m.  Archive predates the E -> IDEN
   rename and the ==-sweep, so it records the argument rather than running.
- `nn-zero-le` -- *warrant well-known:* 0 is the least natural number.
- `co-le-lt-trans` -- *warrant well-known:* a<=b then b<c gives a<c.
- `nn-add-succ` -- *warrant reference:* The recursion equation defining + on NN: a + succ(b) = succ(a + b).  Peano.
   ASSERTED because number-systems.scm axiomatizes NN's + by its algebraic laws
   (comm/assoc/distrib/zero) and never relates it to succ, leaving the two
   symbols unconnected.  Retire it by constructing + as the recursion it is.
- `nn-not-le-succ-le` -- *warrant well-known:* NN is totally ordered and discrete: not(m<=n) gives n<m, hence succ n <= m.

### pivot-clears-col  *(trust: well-known)*

*trusted code: `crs`* -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

proven modulo:

- `entry-in-carrier` -- *warrant well-known:* The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
- `gauge-is-degree` -- *warrant proof:* GAUGE(s) = CHOICE(EUCLIDEAN-GAUGES s) is in FUN(CARR s, NN) and has division-
   with-remainder: IS-EUCLIDEAN-RING(s) makes EUCLIDEAN-GAUGES(s) inhabited
   (euclidean-ring-has-gauge + gauges-mem-build), so the epsilon pick lands in
   it (choice-axiom) and the SEP slices give both conjuncts.  MACHINE-PROVEN in
   archive/calculus-pre-rename/gauge-proof.scm.
- `ring-neg-in-carr` -- *warrant well-known:* NEG closes on the carrier (op NEG CARR CARR).
- `elem-g-type` -- *warrant reference:* ELEM-G(A,n,r,k,l) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A when r in CARR A (entries are
   ONE/ZERO/r, all in CARR A; matof-in-mat).
- `matmul-entry` -- *warrant reference:* (P Q)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij}*Q_{jc}, the product summed in A's additive
   group over j in 1..n (entry-of-matof on the MATMUL tabulation).
- `interval-card-in-nn` -- *warrant well-known:* INTERVAL(a,b) is finite, so its cardinality is a natural number.
- `matprod-summand-type` -- *warrant well-known:* j |-> P_{ij}.Q_{jc} is a function [1,n] -> CARR A for P:MAT(m,n), Q:MAT(n,nn)
   (entry-in-carrier gives both factors in CARR A; MUL closes; CARR(RAG)=CARR A).
- `elem-g-entry-l-vanish` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{jc} = 0 when c=l and j is neither l (diagonal) nor k (r-slot).
- `ring-mul-zero-right` -- *warrant well-known:* a.0 = 0: symmetric to ring-mul-zero-left.  Lang, Algebra II.1.
- `ras-id` -- *warrant proof:* identity of a ring's additive group is the ring's zero.
- `finsum-two-support` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(j)=0 for every j in the finite S except j in {i0,i1} (i0/=i1), then
   FINSUM(ag,f,S) = f(i0) * f(i1) in ag.
- `elem-g-entry-l-at-l` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{lc} = 1 when c=l (the diagonal entry of column l).
- `elem-g-entry-l-at-k` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{kc} = r when c=l and k/=l (the r-slot in column l).
- `ras-op` -- *warrant proof:* operation of a ring's additive group is the ring's addition.
- `elem-g-entry-off-l-off-diag` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{jc} = 0 when c/=l and j/=c (off the diagonal, off the r-slot).
- `finsum-single-support` -- *warrant well-known:* If f(j)=0 for every j in the finite S except j=i0, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=f(i0).
- `elem-g-entry-off-l-diag` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{cc} = 1 when c/=l (the diagonal, r-slot inactive).
- `matmul-type` -- *warrant reference:* MAT(m,n,CARR A) x MAT(n,k,CARR A) -> MAT(m,k,CARR A): the tabulated product
   is a matrix whose entries are FINSUMs in the carrier (matof-in-mat +
   finsum-type).
- `identmat-type` -- *warrant reference:* IDENTMAT(A,n) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A (its entries are ONE/ZERO of A).
- `entry-of-identmat` -- *warrant reference:* (IDENTMAT A n)_{ij} = 1 if i=j else 0 (entry-of-matof on the Kronecker delta).
- `elem-g-col-l` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{il} = 1 if i=l, r if i=k, else 0 (column l: diagonal + the r-slot).
- `elem-g-col-k` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{ik} = 1 if i=k else 0, when k/=l (column k is an identity column).
- `ring-mul-zero-left` -- *warrant well-known:* 0.a = 0: a.0 = a.(0+0) = a.0 + a.0, cancel a.0 in the additive group.  Lang, Algebra II.1.
- `ring-one-in` -- *warrant well-known:* ONE lies in the carrier.
- `ring-add-right-id` -- *warrant well-known:* a + 0 = a (ring-add-left-id + comm).
- `ring-add-right-inv` -- *warrant well-known:* a + (-a) = 0 (ring-add-left-inv + comm).
- `elem-g-col-other` -- *warrant reference:* G[r,k,l]_{ic} = 1 if i=c else 0, when c/=l (any non-l column is identity).
- `matrix-entry-extensionality` -- *warrant well-known:* Two m-by-n matrices over X with equal entries are equal (tuple/list
   extensionality applied row- and entry-wise).
- `ring-neg-neg` -- *warrant well-known:* -(-r) = r in any ring: r's additive inverse's inverse is r (group double-inverse).
- `identmat-left-identity` -- *warrant proof:* I_m P = P: the (i,c) entry is sum_j delta_ij P_jc, whose only surviving term
   (j=i) is 1*P_ic = P_ic (matmul-entry + entry-of-identmat + finsum-single-
   support + ring-mul-left-id).  Proof structure worked out in identmat-build.scm.
- `nn-succ-le-antisym` -- *warrant well-known:* succ a <= b => not(b <= a) for a,b in NN: succ a <= b gives a < b, so b <= a
   would give a < a.  Elementary order on NN.
